# Backend gaps

What a screen needs that the backend doesn't serve. A screen is never reshaped
to fit the current API; the gap is written here, with the artboard and field,
and becomes (or joins) a backend task.

The analysis already turned 136 gaps into tasks (see `tasks/INDEX.md` and
`docs/analysis/ANALYSIS-EVIDENCE.md`, "Backend build list"). Add new ones
below as screens are built.

| # | artboard | field or behaviour needed | found by | task |
|---|----------|---------------------------|----------|------|
| G-1 | all | The backend contract repeats inline response shapes per route (`FlutterwaveConfig` ten times, `Items` four times) instead of named schemas, so clients cannot share one type. Publish named schemas. MONEY-04 did this for the wallet routes (every `/money/*` body is named, checked by `src/money/tests/money-contract.spec.ts`). Still open for the served routes: the Flutterwave checkout config inline 9 times, Prisma rows inlined (a legal request 14 times), `Paginated<T>` inlined 12 times, and 11 nullable named fields written `{ $ref, nullable }`, which generated clients read as never null (list in wawu-backend `docs/contract/WALLET.md`, section 5). Retypes live routes' contract entries, so it needs a V3 run. **LEGAL-01:** the chat-first legal routes (`/legal/assistant/*`, `GET /legal/ops/intakes/{id}/assistant`) are new and publish only named schemas: the matter options the assistant offers are `LegalAssistantTopic` (`GET /legal/assistant/topics`), the conversation `LegalAssistantThread`, its messages, taps, brief and consultant each a named schema, and a nullable named field is written `allOf` so it reads as nullable. The older legal routes are unchanged. | setup, 1 Oct 2026 | MONEY-04 (wallet routes); served routes: none yet (owner to assign) |
| G-2 | all (sign-up) | wawu-id `POST /auth/register` returns an `accessToken` (and refresh token) before the email is confirmed, so the Hub accepts calls from an account whose email was never proven; only `/auth/login` checks `emailVerified`. | G-6 | Settings (sign out) | wawu-id has no sign-out route. | G-10 | W39 (earnings), Z6 to Z8 (delete account), refunds | A frozen Fintava wallet can neither send nor receive: a wallet-to-wallet send into it answers `400 "Kindly confirm both customer accounts are active"` (OPS-02, `docs/fintava/sandbox/15-freeze-unfreeze.md`). So earnings (R-11) and refunds (R-19) sent to a frozen wallet are refused, not held, and the 30-day deletion window (R-17) cannot rely on freezing alone if money may still arrive. Money due to a frozen wallet waits in WAWU's merchant wallet and is paid on unfreeze, or the window does not freeze. No ruling changes; each task handles the case. **MONEY-18 (10 Oct 2026):** a release or a refund into a frozen wallet is refused by the provider (Fintava `400 "Kindly confirm both customer accounts are active"`, kind `wallet_inactive`); the hold stays `held`, the money stays in WAWU's account, `waiting: recipient_frozen`, and the sweep tries again hourly until the wallet is unfrozen (no review bound, nothing is refunded instead). A person with no wallet is `waiting: payee_wallet` or `payer_wallet`, tried every minute. WALLET-16 and SETTINGS-06 still handle their own halves. **SETTINGS-06 (16 Oct 2026, branch `wmt/SETTINGS-06`):** the deletion window freezes the Naira wallet through the provider seam (`freezeWallet`/`unfreezeWallet`, Fintava adapter; Nuvion has no freeze yet, so under Nuvion nothing is frozen), and a cancel unfreezes it. Money due to the frozen wallet waits in WAWU's account and is paid on unfreeze by the MONEY-18 hold sweep (and WALLET-16's earning sweep once it merges). The deletion is also refused up front while a hold is open or earnings wait, and the final purge re-checks the money and keeps the account if any appeared (`AccountDeletion.purgeBlockedBy`), so the freeze is not the only guard. | OPS-02 builder (re-run), 2 Oct 2026 | MONEY-18 (holds and refunds), WALLET-16 (earnings landing), SETTINGS-06 (deletion window) |

| G-16 | sign-up, sign-in (phone lookups) | `register` stores the phone exactly as typed and checks duplicates exactly, so the same number can sit on two web accounts (`0803...` and `+234...`), and any lookup by phone then finds one of them arbitrarily. A data gap, not a sign-up defect (R-36): sign-up refuses a number held by any spelling, but the existing double rows are not merged. Unblocked when AUTH-05 decides which row keeps the number and merges or releases the other. **AUTH-05 (default, agent; owner may override):** the row that keeps the number is (1) the one that proved the phone, else (2) the one that signed in most recently (newest refresh token), else (3) the oldest; every other row in the set releases it (its phone becomes `released:<its id>`, as anonymised rows get `deleted:<id>`; email, password and everything else stay, so it still signs in by email). wawu-id `scripts/data/g16-duplicate-phones.mjs`: `report` only reads; `apply --owner-approved` logs each old phone in `g16_phone_releases` in the same transaction; `revert --owner-approved` puts them back (refuses if any is held by another row by then). Tested on a local scratch database only (3 sets, 4 rows released, revert byte-identical); NEVER run on live data. Unblocked when the owner runs `report` on production, reads it, and approves or changes the rule. **AUTH-05 fix round 1 (verifier U1):** the rule never releases an account whose phone is its only way in (no email, no password, or an email never confirmed): such a row is preferred as the keeper, and when two of them share a number the other is listed by `report` under "owner to decide" and left alone by `apply`. `revert` refuses when the number is held, in any spelling, by a row other than the set's recorded keeper and the rows being restored. wawu-id never hands the `released:<id>` marker on as a phone: tokens and the account answer carry an empty phone, and no reset code is sent to it (`src/auth/released-phone.ts`). Scratch run: 5 sets, 5 released, 1 held, a clashing revert refused with nothing changed, the real revert byte-identical. Real data stays an owner stop. | AUTH-03 verifier (F-15), 2 Oct 2026 | AUTH-05 designed it; the owner runs it (rewrites real user rows: WORKFLOW section 10) |

Fintava's real success body will almost certainly carry more than that, so the expected result today is that every selfie Fintava matches answers 503. Unblocked when a sandbox selfie on a record Fintava matches returns its 2xx (questions 6 and 11 in `docs/fintava/naira-api.md`): then the allowlist is set to that exact body (its verdict field, and its score if it has one). Rows whose figures disagree with Fintava's (found by the sweep, carried by a delivery, or a delivery that Fintava's own record contradicts), or whose status Fintava gave both ways, also stay with `discrepancy` set: that sighting never settles the row (round 2 aligns MONEY-10's merge with the stop rule), a difference landing on a `completed` row puts it back to `pending`, a row holding one is never completed later, and the sweep no longer asks about them (round 3: the same end whichever arrives first). A reversal is applied only with the row's amount, fee and total to the kobo (Fintava's `amount`, `charges`, `total`; one left out counts as a difference) and only to a `pending` send or one Fintava failed; one with other figures (Fintava keeping its fee included), one for a `completed` send, for a send failed as absent, or a second reversal under another `reversalRef`, is written on `discrepancy` and moves nothing (round 3). MONEY-07 now stores every distinct delivery (keyed on the SHA-256 of its bytes), so Fintava sending two different bodies for one transaction with one status reaches the ledger and is recorded too. **Every bank send whose sender records a fee other than Fintava's `charges` will stop** (pending, noted, never settled): the merge compares `feeKobo` and `totalKobo` with the delivery's `charges` and `total`, and the status check cannot see a fee. But A26 (KYC-05) sends them once, to the BVN check, and nothing on the phone keeps them after that request; KYC-03 does not re-ask for them (the brief: never re-ask, never store). So KYC-03 sends `{ image }` to the match and `{ firstName, lastName, dateOfBirth, address }` to the opening (`src/features/open-wallet/api/opening.ts`), and today's Hub refuses both with `400` (`forbidNonWhitelisted` validation, `bvn` missing): no account can open from the app. Needed, one of: (a) the Hub takes the BVN and the NIN from the person's passed check: it holds them encrypted (not hashed; a key outside the database, like `IDENTITY_HASH_KEY`), tied to that check (`bvnVerifiedAt`, `bvnHash`), deleted when the account opens, when another check passes, and after a PROVISIONAL 24 hours, never logged or answered; both DTOs drop the fields (the selfie compares against `checkedIdentity` as the opening already does). Or (b) the BVN check answers an opaque sealed handle (the numbers encrypted under a server key, with the owner and the check's time), which the app holds in memory beside the prefill (`keptCheck.ts`, forgotten on every session end) and sends back instead of the numbers. Either keeps the numbers off the phone after A26; (a) changes KYC-01's storage rule ("never the full BVN"), so it is the owner's call. | G-198 | H33, H36 (which reports are counted) | `POST /ads/:id/events` counts a report only if GET /ads could serve THAT card to THIS viewer right now, by the one shared rule (`servableCampaigns` in `src/ads/ads-serving.ts`): placement, status `scheduled` or `live`, `startsAt <= now < endsAt`, a creative, the card's event open (published, not called off, not over) and its host not blocked either way with the viewer. So it is "servable to this viewer now", not "the placement's current winner": a heavier booking made since does not stop the card on screen being counted. Anything else is one plain 404 that says nothing about why, including a tap that arrives after the event closed. There is no grace after the end: a report that reaches the server after the window closed, an admin paused the campaign or the event closed is not counted. A tap needs no earlier view, so a client that never sends views could make the tap rate above 100%; the rate is `null` when there are no views. **Known edge F1 (verifier):** a report already in flight when a campaign is paused or its event cancelled can still be counted after the pause committed (reproduced with a table lock; every report that STARTS after the pause is refused). The one-statement fix would be a `FOR SHARE` lock on the campaign row inside the insert's transaction, so that a pause (which locks the campaign row) waits for reports in flight; behaviour is NOT changed, the owner decides. **U4 (verifier):** a lighter booking that is permanently outranked can be inflated by any account, because the server keeps no record of what it served (only a signed or recorded serve could fix that). Unblocked by: the owner saying whether a late report (the card was on screen when the window closed or the event closed) should count and for how long, whether only the placement's current winner should count (stricter), whether F1 should be closed with the lock, and whether outranked bookings must be protected from inflation (U4). | ADS-05 builder, 6 Oct 2026 | owner |

After a purge the totals can be higher than the surviving raw rows; `AdsCountsService.reconcile` reports that as a difference by design. A campaign that has any counts cannot be deleted (the foreign keys are RESTRICT); it is ended or paused. The data export leaves `AdEvent` out (listed in `EXPORT_EXCLUDED` with this reason, as `data-export-map.spec.ts` requires): which cards a person opened is an advertising count, not their content. | G-268 | Z6 to Z8 (delete account), sign-out, suspension | Account cases push does not cover. A deletion asked for IS covered since round 2 (lead ruling, 7 Oct 2026): `DELETE /account` deletes the person's tokens and queued pushes and marks the account (`PushStoppedAccount`), so nothing is pushed to it and a token it registers afterwards is not stored; the purge deletes the mark. (a) The Hub keeps no suspension: a person WAWU ID suspends keeps their tokens and keeps getting pushes for notifications that are still written, until they sign out or the account is purged (a purge deletes `PushToken` and `PushDelivery`, `account-data-map.ts`). (b) A phone is cleaned up on sign-out only if the app calls `DELETE /push-tokens` (INBOX-21) while it can still reach the server (a forced end, a refresh token refused or a live-feed close 4403, leaves the phone registered the same way: the Hub holds no session state and cannot tell when WAWU ID ends one, so it needs a WAWU ID to Hub signal; INBOX-21 finding 4, ruled no in SWEEP-INBOX): a phone signed out offline keeps its token under the first person until another person signs in and registers it (the token then moves, and the first person's earlier notifications are not sent to the second), or until Expo reports it dead. (c) A person's switches are per account, not per phone: every phone they own gets every push. (d) `DELETE /push-tokens` carries the token in a JSON body (kept out of URLs and access logs); some HTTP clients and proxies drop a DELETE body. The app's `fetch` sends it; if a proxy in front of the droplet drops it, sign-out answers 400 and the phone keeps its token until another person registers it or Expo calls it dead. Default (agent), owner may override. Unblocked by: WAWU ID telling the Hub about a suspension and a sign-out (a purge or a token delete call from wawu-id), and the owner saying whether a phone may mute its own. | INBOX-03 builder, 7 Oct 2026; round 2, 8 Oct 2026 | owner; wawu-id (owner to assign) |

The success path is tested against stand-in bodies that are labelled guesses. Fintava charges ₦23.25 on a balance transfer below ₦5,000 and ₦15.75 from ₦5,000 (`docs/fintava/fees.md`); WAWU's 15% of a price P is 0.15 P and the release costs the charge, so WAWU's take is 0.15 P minus ₦23.25: ₦0.00 at ₦155.00 exactly, minus ₦8.25 at ₦100.00, minus ₦15.75 at ₦50.00, minus ₦21.75 at ₦10.00, and the same ₦23.25 is lost on a refund at any price (the buyer already paid the charge on the original payment, R-10, and WAWU's 15% never moved). From ₦5,000 the charge is ₦15.75 and the break-even is ₦105.00, so it only bites below that. Nuvion's stand-in bands (₦15 and ₦25) put the break-even at ₦100.00 and ₦166.67. Nothing in the code floors a price: each selling feature owns its minimum. No code in MONEY-18. Unblocked by an owner ruling: a minimum price per held kind (about ₦155), a fee on small holds, or accepting the loss. (4) The history walk is twenty pages of 100 and starts ten minutes before the send; a sender with more transfers than that in the window is `history_incomplete`, never `absent`. | G-700 | W39 (earnings), W26 (an earning row) | A refund or a reversal of an ordinary sale after its earning landed has no rule. What the code does: a sale the provider REVERSES, or that a person puts in review, BEFORE its earning is sent is stopped (the lease needs a completed sale with no review mark; the earning goes to review and nothing moves); after the earning LANDED, a reversal, a payment flagged "paid twice" (G-446) or a refund leaves the creator's wallet as it is, and no clawback exists. The same holds when the provider reverses the earning's own move after it landed (Nuvion's `outflows.refunded`, G-483 for holds): the ledger rows reverse, the earning stays `landed` with its sale's `payeeSettledAt`, and the money is back in WAWU's account while the creator's history shows a reversed row. Numbers: a ₦1,000.00 unlock reversed after landing leaves ₦850.00 with the creator and costs WAWU that ₦850.00 plus the ₦23.25 of the move, because the buyer got the price back from WAWU's account. | G-FIX-80-1 | the nightly check (no screen) | **Done by FIX-80, round 2 (backend `wmt/FIX-80`, 18 Oct 2026), answering the FIX-80 check's F1 and F3:** WAWU's account balance is compared from one provider statement line. A history read to the account's first line compares from before that line over the whole ledger. A longer one is cut at the newest completed statement line from before the window (its running balance is the opening, and its two completed neighbours must follow from it); the ledger side is the merchant rows whose provider line comes after the cut, matched by reference, plus rows with no provider line (pending ones of any age; the others when no older than the cut line). No quiet stretch is needed, and a row dated hours from its line lands with its line. A reversed row whose debit is before the cut and whose money came back after it counts as the money coming back (a bank returning a payout days later no longer reads a false difference). When WAWU's account is still not compared (the statement gives no balance to start from, the pages ran out, or its ledger kept moving) the `balances` check says so and the night is `incomplete`, never `clean`. | FIX-80 builder, 18 Oct 2026 | |

The fix is 12.2.3, which is ESM-only: the droplet runs Node 22 (`deploy/provision.sh`), so it can be loaded, but the Nest build and jest are CommonJS and every container is re-read by a new parser.
